fault.

7.

in the wizard.

in the wizard.

in the wizard.

connection issues indicated through the wizard.
5.

1.

GWC 000639.14 ΓÇö No CAN Bus Messages are Arriving
The controller area network (CAN) binary unit system (bus) has encountered an issue.
The control unit has a communication fault.
Schematic:
Implement CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30D)
See & Spray CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30L)
Diagnostic Steps:
Related information:
Implement CAN Bus ΓÇô Electrical Schematic( Section 240, Group 30D)
Implement CAN Bus ΓÇô Electrical Theory of Operation (Section 240, Group 20D)
Overview: CAN and LIN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Schematic (Section 240, Group 30D)
Overview: CAN Bus and LIN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation (Section 240, Group 20D)
Verify that the diagnostic trouble code (DTC) is still active. Refer to Recall, Record, and Clear Codes
(Section 211, Group 05). If inactive, refer to 
 (Section
Intermittent Diagnostic Trouble Code Diagnostics
211, Group 05).
1.
Cycle the key switch to reboot the system, clear out error messages, and re-establish proper
communication messages.
2.
Verify the gateway control unit (GWC) is communicating on the applicable network or subnet.
3.
Refer to the display's Diagnostic Center application > applicable CAN network (when available).
1.
Refer to the display's Diagnostic Center application > Controllers > and verify that message
counts are changing on all control units along the applicable network or subnet.
2.
Refer to the appropriate schematic and visually check all connectors, components, and harnesses for
corrosion, binding, and damage.
4.
Identify and disconnect all CAN bus passive terminators, located on the appropriate CAN bus network.
5.
Each network should have 2 terminators.
1.
Verify that each terminator has 120 ╬⌐ of resistance across it.
2.
If a terminator does not have 120 ╬⌐, replace that terminator.
3.
Reprogram the control unit or control units connected to this network or subnet. Refer toControl Unit
Reprogramming(Section 245, Group 10C).
6.
If power and ground are good and reprogramming was successful but the diagnostic trouble code
(DTC) returns, replace the control unit.
7.

1661

Overview: CAN Bus and LIN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation (Section 240, Group 20D)
NOTE:
The xx value at the end of the TM number is a language code. If you are unsure of your
language code, use the last two digits of the manual you are currently in.
If your language code is not available, use 19 for the two-digit code for the English version.

Overview: CAN Bus and LIN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation (Section 240, Group 20D)
Test for voltage between the power circuit and the ground/return circuit.
6.
If the test between the power circuit and the ground/return circuit is bad, test for voltage
between the power circuit and a frame ground.
1.
If the tests show the ground/return circuit is bad but the test with the frame ground is good, then
the issue is on the power circuit.
2.
If the test with the frame ground is bad, then the issue is on the ground circuit.
3.
Refer to the electrical schematic. Narrow down any circuit issues to the root cause. Refer to Circuit
 (Section 240, Group 10).
Checks
7.
Reprogram the control unit. Refer toControl Unit Reprogramming(Section 245, Group 10C).
8.
Refer to the electrical system diagnostic.
9.

PDU 002001.09 ΓÇö Source Address 001 Message Missing
The communication system message is missing.
No controller area network (CAN) messages were received from source address 1 within the timeout
period. Restart the engine to attempt recovery.

Operator Presence ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation
The seat is equipped with an operator presence system. Operator presence system is a safety feature and
various functions do not operate due to the lack of operator presence.
Component Information:
The operator presence switch (OPS) is a normally open contact switch on a plate on the underside of the
operatorΓÇÖs seat.
When the operator is in the seat, the seat switch closes the circuit and allows current to flow. With no
operator in the seat, the seat switch is open. This absence of voltage indicates to the operator station
domain control unit (OSDC) that there is no operator in seat. CAN bus message notifies the other control
units that operations cannot be performed until operator presence is detected. If the operator leaves the
seat for more than 5 seconds with the ground drive operating, ground drive stops. If the operator leaves
the seat for more than 5 seconds with any of the implement functions ON, these functions stop as well. To
restart implement functions, the operator must return to a seated position and restart auxiliary or
implement functions.
For additional information on the operatorΓÇÖs seat, refer to the appropriate OperatorΓÇÖs Manual (OM).
Related Information
Operator Seat Presence ΓÇö Electrical Schematic(Section 240, Group 30C)
Overview: CAN Bus and LIN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation (Section 240, Group 20D)
Grounds: Battery and Chassis ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation (Section 240, Group 20G)
OSDC ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation (Section 245, Group 20)

ΓÇó

ΓÇó

[TOC-ANCHOR:sec-09001faa85a1b53e]
Overview: CAN Bus and LIN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Theory of 
Operation
Each controller area network (CAN) binary unit system (bus) transfers messages between various control
units on the machine. These messages include commands, requests, broadcast information, responses,
acknowledgment, and various group functions. The CAN bus is a linear network and the control units are
connected along its length.
Multiple CAN bus systems connect together on each machine. The operator has no direct control of the
CAN bus systems. The CAN bus operates automatically whenever the key switch is in the RUN or START
position.
CAN Circuits
Information, in the form of digital messages, is transferred from control unit to control unit along two
circuits. One control unit sends out a message and the message is either addressed to another control unit
or to all control units. The receiving control unit uses the information in the message to monitor or control
the machine.
Each CAN bus network includes a 2.5ΓÇö3.5 V CAN bus high circuit (yellow wire) and a 1.5ΓÇö2.5 V CAN bus
low circuit (green wire) twisted together. When no messages are being transmitted on the CAN bus, CAN
bus high and CAN bus low circuits are both approximately 2.5 V. When messages are being sent between
the control units, the CAN bus high voltage increases and the CAN bus low voltage decreases.
CAN Terminators
Each CAN bus network has two terminators at each end of the CAN bus. Terminators provide the proper
resistance required between CAN bus low and high signal circuits. Terminators provide message
termination at either end of the circuit. This way, messages cannot bounce back through the circuit and
cause false signals.
Each CAN bus terminator is a 2-pin 120 ╬⌐ resistor. These resistors provide termination because neither
power nor ground is supplied at the terminator. These resistors regulate CAN bus high and CAN bus low
circuits, verifying that each circuit stays within the appropriate range.
All CAN bus networks have terminators but not all CAN bus networks have active terminators. The
implement CAN bus utilizes an active CAN bus terminator. The active CAN bus terminator is a 6-pin or 9-pin
connector. Active CAN bus terminators use machine power to provide 12 V power and ground to two extra
circuits: CAN power and CAN ground. These circuits provide active voltage regulation and protection
against changes in the magnetic field. This protects the CAN power and CAN ground circuits from reading
these magnetic field changes as false signals.
LIN Bus
The local interconnect network (LIN) bus is a single-wire communications protocol between multiple
modules and a control unit. Information is transfered between the modules and the control unit. The
control unit functions as a gateway, connecting information from the modules to other CAN bus circuits.
CAN Networks
This machine has multiple CAN bus and LIN bus networks:
CommandARM (ARMC) LIN bus
1.
Heating, ventalation, and air conditioning (HVAC) LIN bus
2.

ARMC LIN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation
The CommandARM control unit (ARMC) local interconnect network (LIN) binary unit system (bus) facilitates
information transfer between the armrest-located module on the armrest.
Component Information:
Information is transferred to the ARMC from the house control module and the navigation bar on the
ARMC LIN bus 1 circuit. The ARMC LIN bus 2 circuit transfers information from the settings control module
to the ARMC. Information is transferred from the drive control unit to the ARMC on the ARMC LIN bus 3
circuit.
The ARMC monitors and detects switch activation on the modules via the appropriate ARMC LIN bus. Once
the ARM receives switch activation information, that information is transferred on the operator station
domain (OSD) CAN bus system to the appropriate control units.
Related Information:

HVAC LIN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation
The heating, ventilation, and air conditioning (HVAC) local interconnect network (LIN) binary unit system
(bus) facilitates information across the HVAC LIN bus circuit from HVAC components to the auto
temperature control unit (ATC)
Component Information:
The ATC receives temperature information and utilizes it to determine when the various motor assemblies
should be activated. This information is communicated through the HVAC LIN bus. Each motor assembly
adjusts position when commanded.
The ATC functions as a gateway, connecting the HVAC LIN bus and the operator station domain (OSD) CAN
bus.
The ATC communicates with the circulation motor, electrical water valve motor, and HVAC air mode
actuator motor through the HVAC LIN bus.
Related Information:

Overview: CAN Bus and LIN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation (Section 240, Group 20D)

Implement CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation
The implement controller area network (CAN) binary unit system (bus) allows control units to send and
receive messages to and from one another using a two-wire system with two terminators. The two-wire
system provides the implement CAN bus high and implement CAN bus low signals.
Component Information:
The premium server, MTG, MNC, and SA port function as a gateway to connect the implement CAN bus and
vehicle CAN bus. The BCHU acts as a gateway to connect the implement CAN bus and BoomTrac CAN bus.
The OSDC acts as a gateway to connect implement CAN bus, vehicle CAN bus, operator station domain
(OSD) LIN bus, and operator station domain (OSD) CAN bus.
Related Information:

OSD LIN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation
The operator station domain (OSD) local interconnect network (LIN) binary unit system (bus) monitors and
detects switch activation between the operator station control unit (OSDC) and the minipod assembly.

information to the OSDC through the OSD LIN bus.
The OSDC functions as a gateway, connecting the OSD LIN bus with the OSD CAN bus, vehicle CAN bus,
and implement CAN bus.

Vehicle CAN Bus ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation
The vehicle controller area network (CAN) binary unit system (bus) allows control units to send and receive
messages to and from one another using a two-wire system with two terminators. The two-wire system
provides the vehicle CAN bus high and vehicle CAN bus low signals.
Component Information:
The premium server, SA port, and MTG function as a gateway, connecting vehicle CAN bus and implement
CAN bus. The PODC acts as a gateway to connect vehicle CAN bus and powertrain domain (POD) CAN bus.
The OSDC acts as a gateway to connect vehicle CAN bus, implement CAN bus, and operator station domain
(OSD) CAN bus.
Related Information:

PAGE 4360
######################################################################
4360
Each CAN bus network has two terminators, located at each end of the CAN bus. Terminators provide the
proper resistance required between CAN Lo and high signal circuits. Terminators provide message
termination at either end of the circuit. This way, messages cannot bounce back through the circuit and
cause false signals.
Each passive CAN bus terminator is a 2-pin 120 ╬⌐ resistor.
These resistors provide passive termination ΓÇö neither power nor ground is supplied at the terminator.
These resistors regulate CAN Hi and CAN Lo circuits, verifying that each circuit stays within the appropriate
ranges.
Each active CAN bus terminator is a 6-pin or 9-pin connector. Active CAN bus terminators use machine
power to provide 12 V power and ground to two extra circuits: CAN power and CAN ground. These circuits
provide active voltage regulation and protection against changes in the magnetic field. The CAN power
and CAN ground circuits protect CAN Hi and CAN Lo circuits from reading these magnetic field changes as
false signals.
CAN Networks
Every machine has several CAN bus networks:
NOTE:
The ExactApply system can operate without the See & Spray system. However, the See & Spray
system cannot operate without the ExactApply system.
Spray CAN Bus (IB3+) ExactApply CAN Bus
Machines equipped with ExactApply (the solution spray nozzle system) are equipped with an ExactApply
CAN bus. The CAN bus connects the following control units to one another:
The MNC functions as a gateway, connecting vehicle CAN bus 1 (VB1), implement CAN bus 2 (IB1), and the
various ExactApply CAN buses (IB3, IB4, IB5, IB6). For more information about the spray CAN bus, refer to
the appropriate ExactApply technical manual.
The ExactApply CAN bus system works with the machine CAN bus system. The machine CAN bus
communicates directly to the ExactApply CAN bus in order to activate the NZCs and the MNC appropriately.
The NZCs are powered by 12 volt circuits. When the NZCs are powered, the MNC communicates to the
NZCs via the ExactApply CAN bus system for system functionality and proper solenoid timing and control.
Because of the number of NZCs on the booms, not all NZCs are on the same CAN bus circuit. The
ExactApply CAN bus is divided up into four subnets. The subnets are:
Vehicle CAN bus 1 (VB1)
1.
Implement CAN bus 2 (IB1)
2.
Boom CAN bus (IB2) (applicable only when BoomTrac Pro is equipped on the machine)
3.
Spray CAN bus (IB3+) (applicable only when ExactApply is equipped on the machine)
4.
Master Nozzle Control Unit (MNC) (when ExactApply is equipped)
ΓÇó
Individual Nozzle Control Units (NZCs) (when ExactApply is equipped)
ΓÇó
Subnet 1 (CAN bus circuit 3914 and 3915 on the far left boom)
ΓÇó
Subnet 2 (CAN bus circuit 3924 and 3925 on the mid left boom to the left center frame)
ΓÇó
Subnet 3 (CAN bus circuit 3934 and 3935 on the right center frame to the mid right boom)

SSSC ΓÇö Electrical Theory of Operation
NOTE:
System 1 uses the secondary solution system control unit (SSSC).
The secondary solution system control unit (SSSC) is located at the left-rear of the machine. The SSSC
monitors and controls many spraying functions such as pump speed, system pressure, valve positions, and
application rates.
Component Information:
Unswitched battery power is provided from the BCHU/PSSC power fuse within the rear mid-chassis load
center.
Switched battery power is provided from the SSSC power fuse within the mid-chassis load center.
The SSSC relays controller area network (CAN) binary unit system (bus) signals over the implement CAN
bus.
Wakeup is provided when the key switch is turned from the OFF position.
Ground is provided by the battery box ground and the solution skid ground.
Related Information:
